in writing. the report also calls for the establishment of a european union-wide network of national centres for the collection and treatment of human remains. we want to promote the added value that can be created by further coordination, but we do not support harmonisation. we therefore do not believe that the report should be accepted in order to set up a specific eu coordination body, but we want to highlight the forms of cooperation that already exist today and that are mentioned in the report. we also do not support the proposal for a european donation card. decisions on the rules on consent applicable to donation and transplantation should be left to the member states, as these are largely based on moral values, which are often locally and/or culturally determined.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the food crisis we are witnessing today risks taking humanity back a decade in the fight against poverty and creating instability and human misery in large parts of the world. i think it is important to state this and also to note that we have not seen the same rapid development in agriculture as in the parts of the world economy which have been globalised. what we see is a failure of regulated agricultural markets, a lack of open trade and regulations that prevent farmers from using our land. of course, if we wanted to, we too could decide to remedy many of the difficult problems we face today. greater use of the available land is good for the fight for a better climate. more crops contribute to both biofuels and food. but it is also an opportunity to open up economic development in the third world. at the same time, it gives us in europe the opportunity to open up to a more dynamic and open agricultural sector. but the solution to these problems is not more regulation and more subsidies. instead, it is in greater openness for european farmers and farmers worldwide to produce the food and biofuels the world needs. it is a freer agriculture, freer farmers and a free trade. if we can achieve this, we can also give a boost to the international doha negotiations, the free trade negotiations, and at the same time make a contribution to a better climate and a better world.
member of the commission. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the commission, in its communication of june 2007 'sending workers to work in the framework of the provision of services - making the most of the benefits and opportunities while ensuring the protection of workers', identified some shortcomings in the implementation and cross-border enforcement of the posting of workers directive. we concluded at the time that these problems could only be solved if member states improved their cooperation with each other and, in particular, fulfilled their obligations regarding administrative cooperation and access to information laid down in the directive. correct and effective implementation and enforcement are key elements in protecting the rights of posted workers, while lack of enforcement undermines the effectiveness of the community rules in this field. the commission therefore adopted a recommendation on enhanced cooperation on 3 april this year to address shortcomings in the implementation, application and enforcement of existing legislation. in particular, the recommendation calls for more effective exchange of information, better access to information and the exchange of best practices. such enhanced administrative cooperation should improve the protection of working conditions in practice, reduce the administrative burden on undertakings, make it more effective to monitor the fulfilment of existing obligations relating to compliance with working conditions and ensure effective control. the recommendation also proposes the creation of a high-level committee, with the direct involvement of the social partners directly confronted with problems in practice, which would lay the foundation for closer cooperation between labour inspectorates. this committee could be a suitable forum for discussion on a number of issues arising from the implementation of the posting of workers directive. the commission believes that this recommendation provides a basis for intensifying the fight against labour rights abuses and parttime work and that it improves the working conditions of migrant workers throughout the european union. it is now up to the member states to take the necessary steps to improve the implementation of the posting of workers directive. the council is expected to adopt the recommendation at the next employment and social affairs council on 9 june. the commission itself intends to evaluate the implementation of the directive in the light of the latest judgments of the european court of justice, and will work closely with the european parliament in preparing its own-initiative report. the commission is fully committed to the effective protection of workers' rights and will continue its fight against any form of social dumping or failure to respect workers' rights. it should be stressed that there is no contradiction between vigorously promoting workers' rights and promoting a competitive internal market, which provides us with the means to preserve european social welfare. social aspects and competitiveness must be given equal importance if our society and economy are to continue to flourish.
the ppe group is in favour of the report. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. i should like to thank the commissioner for his presentation and i welcome his emphasis on the importance of increased cooperation between member states and the responsibility of the member states to implement the posting of workers directive correctly. i think that there is a point to be made in this debate, and that is that since 2004 we have seen a rapid change in the european labour market. we've got increased mobility. it is important to say that the various fear scenarios that were then circulated regarding the consequences of increased mobility have been shown to be wrong. we talked about social tourism and a host of other problems. the fact is that today there are 1 million europeans stationed in different countries. and in the member states where we have had the greatest openness, we have also had the best developments in the labour market and in wage formation. the posting of workers directive has contributed to better opportunities for individuals but also to the european economy and to the european labour market. a million people! we'll keep that in mind as we discuss three different court cases. because once the court has ruled there, we also see that there are different situations in different cases. but what is also important is that there is nothing in this to say that there is no obstacle to different combat actions in the different member states. is there nothing in the judgments to suggest that they conflict with different types of collective agreements or other forms of pay formation? however, it requires that the member state has legislation and that societies operate in a way that encourages and allows mobility. it is in this context that i also think it is important that we now develop better cooperation and that the various member states make sure that this is implemented and evaluated, so that we combine mobility with social security and safety for everyone in europe. we should not blame the eu for the problems that exist, but we should take responsibility in the different member states and we should welcome the increased mobility and welfare that we can see as an opportunity of the posting of workers directive.
the commission is not prepared to accept this. i welcome the recommendation and i welcome even more the commissioner's desire to work against social dumping and the fact that mr barroso and mr špidla said that the right to strike is not subordinate to freedom of movement. but the proposal you're making is not enough. it is not enough to prevent social dumping. first, let us look at the court's decision in the rüffert and laval case. it has been said that minimum wages are in force, that is to say that a polish worker must work in the same workplace as a german for 46 per cent of what the german works for. if you had said that women should work for 46 percent of what men earn, we would have said that it is discrimination. in this case, it is discrimination against polish workers who are not paid the same as german workers. this is unacceptable. secondly, we do not judge different social models in the same way. in the rüffert case, it is pointed out that it is collective agreements and not the model chosen in lower saxony that are universally valid. the same goes for the laval case. this means that certain labour market models are singled out above others. that is also unacceptable. thirdly, the right to strike is said to be fundamental, but when we weigh it against freedom of movement, it is not very important, not very important at all, but freedom of movement is above it. some speakers have been in favour of 'yes, but we shall have free movement'. of course we should have it, but how do we reward free movement if you dump the conditions in countries? do you think the people in these countries will say, "we welcome people"? no, they're gonna demand that you keep the borders closed. i am in favour of open borders between the new and old member states, but i am also in favour of equal conditions for equal work and that must also be the basis. the commission must therefore act more strongly on changes to the posting of workers directive.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the court of justice is the court of law under the treaties. the treaties must therefore be amended to protect the interests of workers. the court of justice's ruling in laval, viking line and rüffert concludes that the lisbon treaty cannot be accepted by europe's workers without inserting in the treaty a clause on workers' rights - the right to defend collective agreements, the right to take military action to improve wages and conditions of employment. the court ruled that the requirement to pay wages above the minimum wage is now a barrier to trade and that the unions will not be able to prevent wage dumping. commissioner mccreevy replied to me in writing on 17 april this year to a question on trade union rights as follows: trade unions may continue to take action provided that it is justified by legitimate objectives compatible with community law. i can't be more clear. the ecj must therefore be changed. free movement, yes, but without discriminating against employees from other countries.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the court of justice has repeatedly made it clear to the member states that they cannot consider themselves independent in such fundamental policy areas as health policy, gambling policy, alcohol advertising and, most recently, labour market policy. this is what is known as legal activism and must be seen as a direct threat to democracy and the principle of subsidiarity. in its arrogant attitude towards the elected governments of the member states, the court of justice is in effect a self-appointed authority in europe. as regards the laval judgment, it means that swedish and foreign companies will operate under completely different conditions on swedish territory. this is completely unacceptable. i would urge members to think carefully about this the next time they decide to transfer more power to the unelected institutions of the eu. because today it seems the sinner has awoken late.

in writing. − the report contains many proposals on the integration of persons granted international protection. we would have supported these proposals if the vote had taken place in the swedish parliament. in this context, however, the proposals are part of the creation of a common european asylum system. this report is very generous in its approach to refugee policy. at the same time, it is part of building a 'fortress europe'. the protection afforded by the refugee convention is being undermined by the eu taking on the interpretative responsibility of who should be granted protection and what that should look like. we see no alternative but to say no to any form of common refugee policy at eu level, because otherwise the member states will lose control over the direction of the policy. un conventions should remain the guiding instruments for guaranteeing the rights of asylum seekers in the international community.

in writing. − no one questions the importance of the enormous, often successful work carried out by civil society organisations to help people caught up in drug addiction. all good forces need to come together to fight harmful, addictive substances. it was therefore very pleasing to see a report which pays attention to the efforts being made in europe. the reason why i abstained today has nothing to do with the main subject of the report. the reason is instead given under the heading 'civil society' and 'the external dimension'. i voted a while ago, in pain, i must admit, for a report which gave its support to facilitate a conversion to legal ballot papers in afghanistan, where production is to go to painkillers. one reason for this was the number of reports on the negative consequences of aerial spraying of existing crops and the desperate situation of the afghan people. the report calls for both special crops and spraying, which is inconsistent. the report also provides support to organisations in europe that produce substances from cocaine leaves, for example, for 'therapeutic' or 'other legal uses'. i strongly object to this. the main purpose of the report is good, so i decided to abstain.
in writing. the european union, the world's largest donor, must be clear about its values when it acts in the third world. human rights, not only in theory but also in practice, must be a keyword. therefore, to say that the eu should not use the european development fund to promote democracy and human rights by being able to demand development in this area is contrary to our desire to achieve real results. aid is only useful if it benefits the citizens, and then we as donors must be able to make demands to justify the development of democracy, human rights and a functioning civil society. in order to create development, direct aid through peacekeeping operations is sometimes also needed. these should also be covered by the european investment fund, as such actions reduce human suffering and prevent disasters in a very concrete way. of course, in the rich world, we should not impose a particular way of life on others. but we have a responsibility to make choices possible where there are no choices today. i was therefore saddened that the report, in an otherwise good and important report, called into question this principle, which i regard as fundamental, of reciprocity in respect of freedom and human rights and the possibility of peacekeeping operations within the european investment fund.
in writing. − the report by ana maria gomes covers many important aspects, not least the fact that the involvement of the eu and china should be in the interest of the african countries and people, and that external investors operating in africa should respect social and environmental rules. this is why we chose to support the report. however, we do not support the phrasing in the first paragraph highlighting the importance of the treaty of lisbon in enhancing effectiveness and coherence in the eu's external relations.
in writing. - africa is a continent in need of assistance, stronger trade links and greater participation in globalisation that creates more prosperity for all. china has quickly become an important partner on the african continent, with a great appetite for raw materials, not least the oil that several african countries have. the fact that more countries are interested in africa is conducive to its development. however, we must note that china's uncritical approach to access to these raw materials, with little or no regard for human rights, corruption and undemocratic regimes, is a challenge to the eu, which has long advocated combining trade and aid with demands for democratic development with respect for freedom and human rights. the european parliament's clear call for china to take account of human rights and development for the people of africa is an important signal. development is a broader concept than just economic progress and i therefore wholeheartedly support the view that human rights and democracy must be an important part of the relationship in intergovernmental relations, including china's relationship with africa.
for the gue/ngl group. it is estimated that there are some 14 million different species of animals and plants on earth. it says something about what a wonderful planet we have, what a responsibility we have to manage. but biodiversity is under threat. today, more than 30,000 species are threatened with extinction. the main threat is us humans and the economic system we have built, which is based on constant growth and consumption. this is something that you think you can achieve through competition instead of planning, transportation instead of local production. no wonder we are facing a climate catastrophe and a biological depletion. we could reverse this trend. the fact that the eu and its member states have signed the un convention on biological diversity is of course a good thing and can serve as an important tool. we are therefore committing ourselves, for example, to developing action plans to protect biodiversity and to establishing a global network of protected areas on land and at sea, just as an example. but most of this remains to be done at eu level. the very foundations must be attacked, otherwise we will never be able to solve the environmental problems. i want to highlight three areas. the transport madness the eu is built on the idea that no barriers should be erected between member states. a commodity should be produced where it is cheapest. thus, between 1993 and 2000 alone, long-distance rail transport increased by a whopping 30 percent. when the eu and the member states subsidize infrastructure, motorways always get far more than sustainable means of transport. in the former eastern europe, the new member states, the eu is pursuing a veritable asphalt policy with gigantic motorway subsidies. it's the railroad that needs to be stimulated, not the freeways. so to the commission: review the subsidy policy. speaking of subsidies, there is an incredible amount that can be done for agricultural subsidies, 55 billion euros every year. subsidies which are directly negative for the environment should not be paid and agricultural aid should be earmarked for environmental measures and organic farming. biodiversity and climate-smart solutions should be written as a main objective of agricultural policy, not maximum yield. a third fundamental problem that must be tackled is the internal market. i know this is like swearing in church, but the eu simply cannot continue to let the market take precedence over a progressive environmental policy. a few weeks ago, european commissioner günter verheugen replied to me that in the last five years the commission has brought individual countries before the court of justice 19 times in environmental or public health cases. the real scary thing is that in all these 19 cases the court has gone along with the commission, that is to say, the member states have not been allowed to take measures to protect the environment or public health. if we are serious about this, we need an environmental guarantee worthy of the name. we do not have that now and unfortunately we will not have it with the lisbon treaty. that is where market policy will be stuck. in conclusion, the commission has in our resolution we call for sustainability standards for biofuels. i would like to ask, perhaps especially the council: are you prepared to develop not only environmental requirements for biofuels, but also social standards for biofuels to guarantee, for example, sustainable wages, trade union rights, etc. for the biofuels we buy into the union?
mr president, i think that if there is one thing to do to try to halt the loss of biodiversity, it would be to halt the destruction of tropical forests. every year, 14 to 15 million hectares are gone. we have been discussing this issue for years and with no real progress. the real problem is that ecosystem services, whether we are talking about carbon sinks, biodiversity or regulation of the regional climate or hydrological system, have no real value on the market. it is a shortcoming of our economic model, and as long as we do not compensate forest owners for those values – so that it is not more profitable to log forests but keep them there – this rate of deforestation is going to continue. there is a solution: include forests in the emissions trading. the commission, however, is against this, and you have your reasons. the problem is that you provide no alternative, and we all know that there are very limited financial resources in the biodiversity convention – and there are not likely to be any in the future either. i know that the commission cares as much about deforestation in the tropics as the rest of us do, but the problem is: where is your alternative? we need a package. we need a comprehensive approach addressing climate change, carbon sinks, biodiversity and protection of livelihoods of the poor. it would require dg environment and dg development to work closely together. so far that has not happened. please give us an alternative so that we have something on the table and can discuss, otherwise i am afraid deforestation will continue and so will biodiversity loss.
the chairman of the council. - thank you very much for this additional question, mrs panayotopoulos. the presidency is aware that in some regions there is no longer an active population. but, again, it is up to the member states to propose appropriate measures to prevent this from happening. on the other hand, it is also necessary to take into account the fact that no one can be forced to live and work in a particular region. this is a serious problem. it's a problem that needs to be solved. but, again, cohesion policy measures offer solutions, and it is up to the member states to propose them and in what specific cases.
the chairman of the council. - i think it was 20 meters, not five meters, but that's not enough. the commission notes that the airport services agreement with ryanair was signed on 28 june 2010 and that the agreement was signed on 27 june 2010. but i repeat, cohesion policy, the european union's cohesion policy measures, enable the member states to deal with this problem, which mrs panayotopoulos has mentioned. how successful they are at this is another question, but it's possible. but you mentioned postal services, which is a very interesting aspect. however, i would point out that the postal services directive, which was adopted this year together with the european parliament, also addresses this issue appropriately and that an important element of this directive is to ensure the universality of postal services even after the liberalisation of this market. so that question is appropriately addressed in the directive.
the chairman of the council. −the question of ms goudin does not fall within the council's competence, since the council as such did not participate in the intergovernmental conference which drew up the draft treaty of lisbon, but the governments of the member states did. moreover, as is well known, the treaty of lisbon is not yet in force. when it comes into force, and we hope that it will come into force soon, within the prescribed time, the question of the interpretation of its provisions - i think that is the case with mrs goudin's question - the question of the interpretation of its provisions - will be a matter for the court.
madam president, i would like to thank you. i think we should be able to ask questions about how to interpret the treaty which is now being presented under a different name but which is the same, and the council should be able to answer that question. my follow-up question now on question 3 is: does this wording mean that the eu should determine not only the objectives of global environmental policy but also the means, the means that the member states should use to achieve the objective?
it is a good wording that mr goudin uses and it is a great thing that people want to preserve and improve the environment, it is a good wording in the treaty of lisbon. the problem with the eu's environmental policy is that it is always the case that environmental measures are incompatible with the internal market. commissioner verheugen recently said that in the last five years the commission has taken member states to the ecj 19 times in cases where member states have legislated for the environment. in all 19 cases, the court has ruled for the internal market versus the environment. i wonder: does this mean that the environmental guarantee is strengthened in any way? can we say that the environment is superior to market requirements with a wording like this? can you comment on this, please?
the chairman of the council. - i cannot agree with you, mr. dillen, about the double standards. i do not think the european union has a double standard. if they did, the slovenian presidency would not have addressed the issue you raise in your question on behalf of the european union at the united nations human rights council in geneva on 25 march. the european union seeks to have the same human rights standards as all third countries. the question is what means to use. well, that depends on the case. and in order to assess what leverage, what measures to put in place, it is essential to consider what the likelihood is that they will have the desired effect.
the chairman of the council. − the council is closely monitoring the situation of civil rights and the rule of law in chad, especially since the chadian authorities declared a state of emergency. as you know, this state of emergency was lifted on march 16th. the council called on president deby to respect fundamental rights and freedoms, to exercise restraint and to release immediately the persons detained. there's only one other person missing at this stage. on 13 august last year, an agreement was reached with almost all the opposition political parties. the council continues to call on the signatories to the political agreement to continue the implementation of the agreement despite the interruption caused by the violent clashes with rebel groups. the continued implementation of the agreement reached also applies to the provisions on the promotion of democratic freedom and freedom of expression. the council joins international efforts to promote this process, in particular to regain the confidence of opposition political parties and civil society. pressure from the european union led to the establishment of a commission of inquiry, in which the european union and the organisation internationale de la francophonie participate as international observers. the commission's tasks will include investigating human rights violations during the conflict and identifying the circumstances surrounding the disappearance of some opposition leaders. the work of the commission was to be completed within three months.

the chairman of the council. − following attacks by chadian rebels from sudan on the capital of n'djamena in late january, the operation commander, general nash, decided on 31 january to suspend the deployment of forces. this decision was taken for two reasons. first, to allow for an assessment of the new political and security situation, and second, to allow for the smooth evacuation of europeans and civilians from other countries. the notification was resumed after 12 days and this break, a 12-day break, did not affect the overall timing of the operation. initial operational capacity was reached on 15 march and full operational capacity is planned to be reached by the end of june. notwithstanding the suspension of the deployment for that short period, the council has made clear that it remains fully committed to the fulfilment of the mandate under the relevant un security council resolution, in particular the mandate on humanitarian aspects. the clashes in early february were envisaged as one of the likely scenarios and this was taken into account from the very beginning in the planning of the operation. unfortunately, this scenario has come to pass, which has further confirmed the importance of the deployment of eufor and the united nations mission in the republic of chad and the central african republic. the council considers that it is in the interest of the international community, and also of the european union, that stability and security in the region be improved. the presence of this multidimensional structure will contribute to stabilising the humanitarian and political situation and prevent the crisis from spreading to neighbouring countries and regions. regarding the incident on the border with sudan in which a soldier of the eufor mission was killed, the operation commander regretted the inadvertent crossing of the border and the fact that this accidental incident had resulted in a fatality. the commander reaffirmed that eufor would carry out its mandate in accordance with the un security council resolution, i.e. impartially and with full respect for the sudanese border. i should add that the investigation into the incident is ongoing. the force's being directed as planned. at the moment, 1,800 eufor personnel are deployed in chad and the central african republic. when fully operational, the mission will include 3,700 troops from 14 member states. negotiations are also under way with third countries on their possible contribution.

member of the commission. − madam president, the commission is grateful to parliament for this insightful and timely report. making sure the wto can respond to the challenges of a rapidly changing global economy should be a major priority for the european union. in addition, in the light of the current economic difficulties that many wto members are facing, a strong multilateral institution is more essential than ever to counter the call for beggar-thy-neighbour protectionist policies. the report recognises the central importance of the doha development agenda (dda). the commission wishes to reiterate that any initiative in geneva with regard to the future of the wto will have to be calibrated and rooted in what we hope will be a successful outcome of the dda. today, during what could be the final phase in the dda negotiations, no wto members would have the political willingness and/or the administrative resources to engage in a meaningful reflection on the reform of the wto. we do expect and hope that this situation will evolve towards a final dda deal to be signed before the end of 2008. this will also open the gates to a debate on the future of the wto, a debate that will be highly dependent on whether the dda is concluded successfully or not. as to the ideas expressed in the report, the commission shares most of them in principle, even if it is clear that some proposals put forward in the motion for a resolution will face strong resistance from certain wto members. with regard to institutional matters, the commission remains supportive of enhancing the parliamentary dimension of the wto and of other ideas, like granting additional resources to the wto secretariat and expanding its tasks, as well as strengthening the wto active transparency mechanisms and to monitor and supervise effectively the application of the rules. we note the call for openness in the dispute settlement procedures and would like to underline that it was based on our initiative, that some hearings in wto cases involving the european union have been open to the public. as to the substantive proposals put forward in the report, the need to integrate non-trade concerns within the scope of wto rules will remain a guiding principle of european union policy within the multilateral framework, but also in the framework of its bilateral negotiations recently launched on the basis of the global europe communication, and unilateral initiatives in favour of developing countries. the new unilateral system of tariff concessions in favour of developing countries, the general system of preferences plus, is an example of that. the relation between trade policy and labour and environment will surely be an important area, and other trade subjects might appear on the agenda. the shaping of a new multilateral policy after the conclusion of the doha round will be one of the most stimulating and difficult challenges for european trade policy. parliament will take on an ever-growing role in designing and driving such policy, in particular once the lisbon treaty enters into force. the commission, and my colleague mr mandelson in particular, is looking forward to engaging with you in open and constructive dialogue in order to reach solid and realistic conclusions aimed at strengthening the wto and the whole multilateral trade system.
draftsman of the opinion of the committee on economic and monetary affairs. − madam president, i would like to thank the rapporteur and the committee on international trade for this report. i think it is worthwhile, when we discuss the wto, also to underline the fact that globalisation and free trade have given millions and millions of people new opportunities. in the last two decades, we have seen more progress in the fight against poverty than the world has ever seen before. this underlines the need to safeguard a stable framework of fair and multilateral rules for free trade. this is the main task of the wto and it is also why it is important to have a strong and strengthened wto. we need to ensure that we can have more international trade and competition without distortions. this is why the committee on economic and monetary affairs has pointed to the need to reduce state aid, to remove non-tariff trade barriers and to help developing countries to be in a better position in the negotiations regarding the multilateral framework and the rules of free trade. this underlines once again why we need a strong wto and a strong secretariat – in order to take decisions and to uphold the rules, but also in order to develop the multilateral trade system. if we succeed in having a strong wto and a strong secretariat, we will do an important thing to safeguard the opportunities for global trade to develop in a framework of free and fair rules. that is our task – nothing more and nothing less. in that sense, it is also important to highlight some of the things that need to be done: we need to increase free trade in services; we need to increase openness in financial services; we need to ensure that we can have more free trade in agriculture. when we discuss increasing food prices, we can see the importance of opening up the markets. but we will never succeed in doing this without a strong wto and without the opportunity to uphold a strong framework that is good for the developing countries, that can give poor people new and better opportunities and that can also help us all benefit from the opportunities of free trade.
on behalf of the ppe-de group. – mr president, this is, as already has been said, an important report and i want to congratulate ms ferreira. we know that our current economic model fails to integrate environmental costs into market prices. already at the stockholm conference in 1972 the ‘polluter pays’ principle was established and agreed upon. but, as we know, it has not been implemented in most countries. this report is very important. it responds to the many market failures we experience in several areas, where of course climate change is the most obvious problem today. the report suggests plenty of initiatives in many key areas. in general most of those proposals are well-drafted. however, i think more effort could have been made by us all to try to shorten the report and merge some of the individual paragraphs; very similar demands are now being put forward. i think the report would have benefited and become much clearer. on behalf of my group and as has already been said by mr purvis, i have to mention that we have difficulties with a few paragraphs. one is paragraph 26. you cannot favour an emissions trading system and at the same time ask for a general co2 tax at european level. it is inconsistent. secondly, we have some problems with paragraph 24. while we agree with most of the contents, it is, in our opinion, too general in its language and somehow gives the impression that auctioning revenues would be or should be part of the eu budget in the future. that is not something we can support. but, apart from that, we are in general in support and once again congratulations to ms ferreira.
for the gue/ngl group. we will shortly be taking decisions on the environment committee and anne ferreira's report on market-based environmental instruments. it is an excellent report and i fully support it. let me make a personal comment first. we're talking about market-based policy. personally, i am tired of all this talk about market control of environmental policy. we don't need more influence for the corporations or the market. we need more political guidance. we need more planning. for example, we need tougher emission standards, we need to be able to use public procurement for environmental purposes and environmental taxes and charges. this is where we really have the market-oriented instruments, because it is precisely about imposing taxes and levies on what aggravates environmental problems, simply internalizing environmental problems. these instruments are a good example of how we are taking political responsibility for environmental problems rather than putting them on the market. let me give you some concrete examples of the most important aspects of this report. a carbon tax, a carbon tax combined with a clear reduction requirement is likely to be the most effective way to bring down emissions. my own country was the first in the world to introduce a carbon tax. it was the early 1990s. without claiming that sweden is a pioneer in all climate areas, the co2 tax has actually been one of the main reasons why we have reduced our carbon dioxide emissions. it's time for more countries to join the fight. in paragraph 26 of this report we call for a carbon tax. overall, we call for increased support for member states to introduce environmentally sound taxes. i think today there is a very unique opportunity to push for a carbon tax and other environmental taxes. in just the last few years, there's been a tremendous increase in consciousness in the world. the report also states that the member states themselves decide on this matter. of course, that's exactly right. it is the competence of the member states. another issue is the emissions trading system. it is only to be admitted that the first trading period 2005-2007 has been nothing but a disaster. the system has failed to reduce emissions and many polluters have even been given generous allocations and made huge profits on this system. in this report we are calling for the auctioning of emission allowances, we are calling for an emission ceiling that is consistent with the 30% reduction target and for limits and strict requirements for the use of so-called flexible mechanisms. in doing so, the trading system will hopefully start to function. if this does not happen, consideration should be given to replacing the trading system entirely with something else, such as high taxes on emissions. in this context, it is important to remember that now that aviation is to be included in the emissions trading scheme, it is not possible to limit the measures against aviation to the trading scheme alone. parallel measures such as a tax on aviation fuel and taxes on nitrogen oxide, nox, are a must, and that is exactly what we are asking for in this report. i would like to ask the commission: you've said before that you're going to present measures against the nitrogen oxide emissions of aviation, against nox, and that it's going to come this year. can you tell me what's going on? do you have a date for when the measures will come? given that aviation has almost doubled its emissions in the last ten years, there are many of us in this room who want to see concrete action. we can't wait any longer! these instruments are therefore a good example of how we are taking political responsibility for climate change. with taxes and fees, we can speed up the necessary emissions reductions and hopefully solve the entire climate problem. let's do this it's our duty to our children and to all other living creatures on this beautiful planet.
mr president, i would like to thank you. environmental issues have rightly become one of the major political issues of our time and they have also rightly become one of the most important areas of eu cooperation. firstly, crossborder environmental pollution in europe cannot be dealt with at the level of the nation state, and this gives the eu an obvious role in such areas. secondly, the eu is the world's largest economy, with a huge production and foreign trade, and the member states agree to take responsibility for the environmental consequences of this fact. this combination of size and consensus on basic environmental objectives gives the eu the potential to exert a global influence of considerable significance. but the report does not inspire confidence. there is no distinction between the consumption of finite resources and the impact on the climate. the rapporteur is also shameless in using the climate issue as an argument for supranationality, bureaucracy and protectionism. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. there is no recognition here that the countries in competition with each other must seek the solutions. and to top it off, the usual boastful claim that we must institute a new way of life in our western societies. that's pathetic. our job is to make sure that the environmental costs of consumption are reflected in the prices consumers pay. free citizens choose their own way of life.
i would like to express my dissatisfaction that we have not had the opportunity today to vote against the proposal for personal carbon dioxide cards, i.e. personal cards where we are expected to measure the contribution of individual individuals to the greenhouse effect. it's just wrong. it is against human activity and development and sends out very strange signals: do not breathe, do not exercise, above all do not flinch and, for god's sake, do not fart! what would be the penalties for those who consume too much carbon dioxide or contribute too much to the greenhouse effect? should we forbid them to breathe at all? no, this kind of idiotic ideas undermines our credibility and takes the focus away from the real, effective means, namely emissions trading, polluter pay principles and other methods that actually work. i am surprised that there are not more people who have protested against this proposal and that it seems to pass unnoticed. i am terribly sorry if in this speech i have contributed to the consumption of more carbon dioxide than i am actually entitled to. i'm sorry about that.
in writing. the resolution contains a number of vague points. we are in favour of the development of a single market. however, the european parliament's expression of regret at the lack of concrete harmonisation in this area can be interpreted in several directions. one of these is that more detailed regulations on total harmonization should be introduced, which we do not support. the proposal also rejects any attempt to renationalize cohesion policy and supports a common immigration policy and the development of a unified nato policy, so we have voted against the joint motion for a resolution.
in writing. - we sympathize with much in this motion for a resolution, but parts of it are matters which are not of concern to this parliament but are matters for which the member states and, if necessary, the council of foreign ministers are responsible. as always, there are irrelevant amendments from different groups on various major issues of principle, such as the inclusion of the mrta in the eu list of terrorist organisations and the attempts at a peace process in colombia. these are important issues, but of such dignity that they should not be decided by the european parliament but by the competent decision-makers in the member states. that is why we voted against the report.

in writing. - we swedish social democrats have voted in favour of this report. we believe that economic instruments must be developed at eu level to meet the many environmental and climate challenges. the report also stresses the principle of subsidiarity. however, we chose not to support a paper on targeted regulation for low-income households. there are simpler and better general measures to offset the social consequences of environmental policy instruments. we also voted against amendments nos 2 and 3. these cover important areas such as the efficiency of combustion engines and the use of pesticides. however, we believe that the best way forward is through parliament's ongoing work on reducing the climate impact of passenger cars and regulating the use of pesticides.
in the light of the above, the commission considers that the proposed amendments to the directive on the protection of the environment and the protection of the environment from harmful substances should be approved by the council. we reject the idea of a 'carbon card' for individuals and small businesses, which would record their energy consumption and greenhouse gas emissions. on the one hand, we believe that the practical difficulties themselves are likely to be an obstacle. we also believe that the proposal is contrary to the right to privacy and freedom, not least because it does not explain why this data is to be collected or what it is to be used for. we also reject the proposal for carbon dioxide tariffs. free trade makes the most efficient use of the earth's resources. the focus should be on international environmental agreements.
in writing. - environmental issues are one of the most important areas of eu cooperation. cross-border environmental pollution in europe cannot be dealt with at the level of the national states. this gives the eu a clear role in such areas. however, it is very worrying that the report shamelessly uses climate issues as an argument to increase the political power of the eu and open it up to bureaucracy and protectionism. there are many examples, all of them wrong. this requires common taxation, a re-examination of the concept of free competition and the introduction of so-called frontier adjustments, i.e. customs duties. the way in which member states deal with environmental policy must be left to the member states themselves. it is in the competition between countries and companies to find effective solutions to environmental problems that creativity can flourish, not by bureaucratizing existence. that is why we voted against the report.
in writing. - as a swedish liberal, i am fundamentally sceptical about common eu taxes. this is not the case with environmental taxes. at the same time, careful consideration must be given to the combination of control instruments that is particularly effective. i am a great believer in emissions trading and am not opposed in principle to a carbon tax. but the balance between them must be well thought out and as the proposals were pitted against each other i thought it appropriate to make a mark. the ferreira report was mixed. however, most of it was positive and i did not have to think long before i voted in favour. but i'll tell you what, it wasn't all good. point 66, which was not put to a separate vote, was for example on the introduction of a 'carbon card' for individuals and smes. politics is certainly something to be desired. but it's better to be feasible, too.

for the ind/dem group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. whenever we come to the eu's agricultural policy in this house, the sensible citizen is reminded of how unreasonable it still is, despite some improvements in recent years. we face constant demands from interest groups for continued protection against foreign competition. the regulatory systems have been in place for so long that they have even distorted the way people think and talk about these issues. that is why the rapporteur can say without a shudder: it is important that community cotton production remains profitable, with production levels high enough to keep the refining industry, which employs 3 200 people in greece and 920 in spain, profitable. if we reasoned like that in other areas, europe would be heading for its demise. the truth is that spain is a very prosperous industrial country with a workforce of some 20 million people. such a country can have no problem in transferring 920 people from cotton-purification to other more productive activities. the rapporteur also uses without hesitation expressions such as 'it is absolutely necessary to preserve cotton growing, which is a profitable part of community agriculture'. this is an extreme example of contradictio in adjecto. common sense says that if cotton farming is profitable, it needs no support. the bitter truth is that cotton production in the eu is unprofitable and should therefore be phased out. such adaptation may be difficult and require state aid, but the aid should then be used to change and not to protect production that is clearly best carried out in other non-eu countries.
mr president, i would like to thank you. europe is one of the least developed regions in the world in terms of lobbying rules, as jens-peter bonde pointed out. however, we can do better today by supporting the amendments which call for a mandatory register of financial interests and for serious sanctions for those who break the rules. we also need to clarify what the externally paid people working in the commission actually do. as many have pointed out, lobbying is not inherently bad, but the problem is that when we centralize decisions, it becomes easy for large companies to convert economic power into political power. at least four-fifths of the lobbyists are paid by big corporations. that imbalance affects our legislation. so, publicity must mean that all changes to legislation submitted by lobbyists must be publicly recorded, so that we can see who is behind them. when the register of those who will be allowed to come here is drawn up, i would ask astrid lulling to ensure that one third of the seats are allocated to companies, one third to non-governmental organisations and one third to the others. then we can have balance.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the report's praise of sport is only something to be agreed with. sports mean a lot to society, and it creates a lot of important values that i can't do justice to in two minutes. it also helps to bring europe together. just because something is important does not mean it is political, and just because something helps to bring europe together does not
