in writing. −initiatives and measures to prevent children from being exposed to crime when using the internet are urgently needed. for this reason, we voted in favour of the report by mrs angelill on the establishment of a multiannual community programme to protect children using the internet and other communication technologies. we would like to stress, however, that a number of the measures proposed in the report are better initiated and financed by the member states themselves. however, other measures aimed at combating, for example, child pornography, should be designed through cooperation between member states, given the global nature of the problem.
in writing. -today there are hundreds of simple, accessible and cheap computer programmes that effectively prevent children from accidentally visiting inappropriate websites on the internet. in addition, most standard browsers have various "child functions" installed, which make it easy for parents to control which pages their children are allowed to visit. the rapporteur is very vaguely proposing that a total of eur 55 million of european citizens' tax money should be allocated to an eu propaganda programme which in our view is unnecessary, expensive and ineffective.
in writing. −i have today abstained in the final vote on the report by mr jan andersson on the challenges facing collective agreements in the eu (a6-370/2008). collective agreements, trade union rights and the right to take military action are enshrined in the lisbon treaty, which i want to make legally binding. there is also the right to work, the right to run a business and the right to move across the union's borders. as the proposal requires, trade union representatives cannot be exempted from the respect of fundamental principles of community law, such as the principle of proportionality. it applies, and of course should apply, to all citizens of the union. the european parliament should be the strongest representative of community law and the treaties. the position adopted by parliament on the legal basis risks becoming a threat to the future of free movement. however, i am very pleased that the european parliament has taken a stand on the swedish model and our collective agreements.
in writing. the social democrats and the liberal group have wholeheartedly supported all the changes to the eu treaties. they have thus helped to give the eu institutions, including the ec court of justice, increasing power over the swedish labour market. the eu has thus become a threat to labour market rules developed through negotiation and legislation with strong roots in swedish society. the report mainly advocates changes to the posting of workers directive. it cannot therefore prevent the court of justice from continuing to intervene in the swedish labour market. firstly, the result is a compromise between the conservatives and the social democrats which has led to a number of flaws and contradictory statements. secondly, the primary eu law on the internal market (article 49) takes precedence over the provisions of the posting of workers directive. the court of justice can therefore still reach the same conclusion as in the laval case. the eu should not be given tasks that the member states can handle themselves, and that is where the labour market really comes in. the junilista therefore advocates a swedish derogation from eu labour market legislation to ensure that the ecj cannot in future govern the swedish labour market. we have nevertheless voted in favour of the report, because the aim is still to try to prevent the ec court from interfering in swedish collective agreements in the future. we have also supported the amendments which advocate more national self-determination in labour market matters, but we have of course voted against the rapporteur's praise of the lisbon treaty.
in writing. - the report by mr jan andersson on the future of collective agreements in europe, following the laval judgment, was voted through. the social democrats have called for the current eu legislation, the so-called expatriation directive, to be scrapped to ensure that sweden can retain its collective agreements. on the other hand, i have been active both in the committee on the internal market and in the plenary to ensure that the issue is discussed primarily at the right level: the national level. the reason is that i think the social democratic strategy is ill-conceived. by repeatedly trying to raise the laval case to a european level, instead of solving it through swedish legislation, pressure is being put on for more common labour market legislation - which is what caused our current problems in the first place. when 27 member states are to agree, it is obvious that it is incredible that sweden in particular would pass legislation that suits us best - our model is after all quite unique in a european comparison. as mr andersson and parliament accepted my proposal not to scrap the posting of workers directive until national investigations had made it clear that it was really necessary, i thought i could still vote for the report.
in writing. −the report on collective agreements in the eu is an opinion without legal value. its intention is to strengthen the position of employees after the laval judgment, but unfortunately the content of the report does not live up to this intention by any means. it is wrong to renegotiate the eu's posting of workers directive as proposed in the report. this could lead to a deterioration of the situation of the workers. it is a risk we are not prepared to take because conservative forces dominate the whole eu system. the report lacks the text that the right to strike should be above the freedom of the market and that this should be included in a socially binding protocol to the lisbon treaty. the left group tabled several amendments to this effect, but they were rejected by a large majority. the swedish government has therefore decided to include in the treaty of lisbon a derogation which states that the effects of the laval judgment do not affect sweden. but this amendment from the left party was also rejected. the report instead praises the lisbon treaty, even though the treaty does not in any way change the law, but only consolidates it.
in writing. i voted against the report. however, additional regulation at eu level would likely strengthen the power of the ec court of justice. in my view, questions in the field of labour market policy should not be decided by the ec court of justice but by the riksdag and/or the social partners. i voted against the positive writing on the lisbon treaty and do not believe that the charter of rights should become legally binding as this risks in practice transferring legislative power from the riksdag to the ec court of justice.
in writing. − we fully support the respect for human rights and democratic principles, as outlined in the eu-vietnam cooperation agreement, and believe that there is a need for improvement by vietnam in this regard. such principles are universal, and should be applied equally in relation to all countries, inside and outside the eu. therefore, we are voting in favour of this resolution, despite the rather unbalanced way in which it is presented.
for the ind/dem group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. this is a budget which is in some ways well-drafted. it is competent and reflective rapporteurs who deserve all the credit for their work within the framework we have. but of course all this is completely wrong, because what is the eu really going to do? here, at least two thirds of the money goes to agriculture and to structural funds, including waste and corruption. what the eu should be investing in, for example, large-scale research like the fusion project in barcelona, or common infrastructure like galileo or carbon capture and storage, etc., is small potatoes in this budget. here we find a globalisation fund which now provides eur 2.4 million to portugal and eur 10 million to spain. in both cases, the figures are fractions of the per mil­lion of these countries' gross domestic products. there is no reason for them to get money from other countries for this. we have a solidarity fund where 13 million euros go to france for the hurricane dean's ravages in martinique and guadalupe. this is again a fraction of the per millimeter of france's gross domestic product. this is completely unreasonable! we are talking about rich countries which can do without any difficulty, scraps from other member states. why is this done? i would argue that this is done because it is publicity money that is to serve as advertising for the eu. it has no other meaning. i guess we're in for a snowstorm. i repeat, we are on the wrong track. we are discussing a budget which is technically well-made but politically idiotic.
mr president, i would like to thank you. as rapporteur for the committee on budgets, i would like to thank mrs jutta haug for listening so closely to our priorities. they're really hitting the budget. to my swedish eu-sceptic colleague nils lundgren who spoke just now and who called the budget idiotic, i just want to say that it is not getting any better by him personally proposing the total closure of some of the best parts of the budget, such as the maritime safety authority and the youth in action programme. but the eu budget has problems. the triple crisis we are living in shows what it is all about. i'm talking about the climate crisis, the food crisis, the financial crisis. the committee on industry proposes at least a 5% increase in all operational appropriations relating to climate and energy, but this is still a drop in the bucket. as a socialist, i see the need for food aid, but the debate on funding shows the weaknesses of the budgetary system. it just goes to show how difficult it is to make more meaningful re-prioritizations. president nicolas sarkozy yesterday pointed out the need for joint action by europe's businesses in the wake of the crisis, but there are no such resources in the eu budget. the committee on industry points to the need for more funding for smes, but even that is a drop in the bucket. instead, we are forced to invent one temporary instrument after another. we have the flexibility instruments, the globalisation fund, the solidarity fund and the food facility and we will soon have a special mechanism to deal with the proceeds from the future auctioning of emission allowances. i believe that it is time for a more ambitious and forward-looking review of the longterm budget. we have the opportunity now to do a mid-term review to make sure it is better adapted to deal with the increasingly complex reality we live in and where radical action is required. (parliament adopted the resolution)
president of the council. - mr president, commissioner, mr olli rehn, mrs pack, madam rapporteur, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by thanking mrs pack very much for the quality of her report and for what she has just said, which will allow me to be more brief on this difficult subject. as you know, the council is very attached to the european perspective of the western balkan countries, and i confirm this today. this movement in favour of european integration in the balkans was launched at the initiative of the french presidency, which remains strongly committed to this objective: it was launched in 2000 when the european union, for the first time, brought together all these countries at the zagreb summit and recognised their vocation to join the union. each of the balkan countries now has such a prospect, which guarantees the stability of the region and the political and economic development of each of the states, and this prospect is becoming more and more meaningful, and in particular with regard to bosnia and herzegovina, which is the country in the region which - should we not forget? - has suffered the most from the conflicts arising from the break-up of the former yugoslavia. but today - as you have said, madam rapporteur - this country is at a crossroads between a european perspective that will lead it to membership of the european union or to withdrawal into itself, based on nationalist rhetoric that looks backwards. the remarkable progress made in the first quarter of 2008 allowed the union to take the historic decision to sign this stabilisation and association agreement with bosnia and herzegovina. it was a sign that with will and determination, the country's political leaders were able to reach a consensus and undertake the necessary reforms. this is the determination that the council wanted to acknowledge when it signed the agreement last june, once the four conditions had been met. i would remind you of these four conditions: good general cooperation with the international criminal tribunal for yugoslavia, reform of public broadcasting, improvement of public administration and the launching of police reform. the signing of the agreement and the interim agreement on 16 june in luxembourg is a particularly important milestone in relations between the union and bosnia and herzegovina. this should give a new impetus to the country's progress towards the european union. it's an impulse that needs to be confirmed without delay. i would remind you that the interim agreement entered into force on 1 july last. it's a first step, but i'd say it's the end of the beginning! there's still a lot of work to be done! that is what we said to the leaders of that country, with commissioner olli rehn, who is here, when we met them on the sidelines of the un general assembly and at the troika meeting. in the key areas of the agreement and the european partnership, we must not only consolidate the progress achieved but, as you have stressed, madam rapporteur, accelerate the reforms. it is extremely important that the momentum of reforms on the rule of law, respect for democratic rules, also on police reform, be much stronger than it is today. let no one tell us that there are problems of division between the political leaders and public opinion in bosnia and herzegovina, because all the surveys show that the european aspirations of the people of bosnia and herzegovina are extremely strong. more than 80% of this population genuinely expect progress towards the european union! what are their political leaders waiting for to meet the legitimate aspirations of the people of bosnia and herzegovina? for our part, within the framework of the union, we are doing what we can to support that country economically and financially, to help it along the way, to help it progress along the road to security, to help it progress along the road to the modernisation of the police with the police missions we have set up, and also the military missions. the experience of recent accessions - and i will end here - shows that efforts on the european agenda are paying off. i would like the political leaders of bosnia and herzegovina to understand that the stabilisation and association agreement provides a solid basis and leverage for a deep commitment by this country. we will not give up on them, we have done our duty. it is up to the political leaders of this country to know what commitments they must make and assume, to ensure what is the only possible way, namely a permanent commitment to closer union with the european union.
member of the commission. − mr president, i would firstly like to thank doris pack for her excellent report. i welcome the opportunity to discussbosnia and herzegovina with you today at a very crucial moment for the country and its european aspirations. the signature of the stabilisation and association agreement (saa) last june was a significant step forward for bosnia and herzegovina. together with the start of the visa dialogue, it gave a clear signal to the people of that country that their future lies in the european union. the eu was able to initial the saa last december and sign it in june because the country’s political leaders pulled together and reached consensus on the main conditions, particularly on police reform. this proves that progress can be achieved and crises overcome when the political will exists, as mr jouyet rightly pointed out. however, this consensus has since collapsed and reforms have been halted. nationalist rhetoric ahead of the local elections in october was one factor in this deterioration. yet the country’s political problems run much deeper than that. the lack of a common vision among the country’s leaders about its future and the absence of consensus on eu reforms seriously harm its european prospects. there is also open disagreement on most political questions, while there is no sense of urgency or responsibility to overcome this political stalemate. i expressed my serious concern to the presidency of the country during my visit to sarajevo a few days ago. i underlined that bosnia and herzegovina now needs to put eu-related reforms at the top of its political agenda and address the priorities of the european partnership, including state- and institution-building. your resolution sends a strong signal to the leaders of bosnia and herzegovina to get on with the reforms and to get their country back on the road to europe. likewise, bosnia and herzegovina must be able to speak with one voice to advance in european integration. another litmus test of the country’s ability to deliver on eu integration is the census. from an eu perspective – as we all know – census data are vital for social and economic planning and development and for most community policies. the commission will offer its assessment on the situation in bosnia and herzegovina in the progress report on 5november. like you, we shall alsopoint out the fact that the leaders of bosnia and herzegovina can either continue to quarrel and fall behind their neighbours, or get on with reform and move forward towards the eu. your resolution underlines that closing down the office of the high representative (ohr) and strengthening the role of the european union should remain our ultimate objective. i agree. the future of the ohr is a matter for the peace implementation council to decide, but it is clearly in the interest of bosnia and herzegovina to reach a point where the ohr will no longer be needed, thus paving the way for a stronger eu presence and for realising the eu perspective of the country. in other words, as we move into the next phase of our relations, bosnia and herzegovina itself must assume full ownership of the reform process that underpins its eu perspective. the signature of the saa this summer provided an opportunity that should not be wasted. the challenge for bosnia and herzegovina’s leaders is to achieve a degree of political consensus such as has delivered progress on eu integration elsewhere in the western balkans region. they have done it before so they can do it again. i hope our messages will now be heard.
the ppe group is in favour of the report. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. in june this year, bosnia and herzegovina signed a stabilisation and association agreement with the eu. the country thus made a steady step forward on its path towards the eu, but the work is far from complete. the eu's commitment cannot and should not be reduced by the belief that everything will now be arranged automatically. a whole range of challenges remains. the constitutional reform has not been implemented. the issue of state property in the brčko districts is still unresolved. there are clearly wide differences of opinion on when and how the office of the high representative should be closed, both within bosnia itself and within the international community. it is my firm opinion that the closure of this office cannot be an end in itself. the aim is, however, to meet the requirements and conditions imposed on bosnia by the peace implementation council, so as to enable the office of the high representative to be transformed into the office of the eu special representative. in this respect, as in a number of other challenges facing bosnia, the unity of the international community is vital. to think that the local parties in bosnia can themselves agree on, say, constitutional reforms is naive, i would say downright dangerous. the new constitution must lead to a strong united state if the country is to continue to move closer to the eu. but the political dialogue in bosnia does not indicate any interest in achieving this. equally dangerous is the belief that the international community's commitment can diminish now that the country has made some progress and the international military presence is steadily decreasing. on the contrary, it is now the eu itself that is becoming even more accountable.some measures, such as making it easier for people to travel and study abroad, and helping to develop democracy and implement the necessary reforms, are just a few examples of other areas where the eu can and should be more active. our commitment and the way we handle the situation in bosnia in the coming months and years will be decisive, not only for the future and security of bosnia but for the whole region.
for the ind/dem group. madam president! together with volvo and ikea, the institution we call the ombudsman is my country's most well-known contribution to the world community. it is a very important institutional innovation, for democracy in general and for the eu institutions in particular. why would you do that? yes, because an ombudsman is supposed to ensure that citizens can assert their rights in political and bureaucratic structures that are becoming increasingly complex and therefore opaque. nowhere in the democratic world is complexity and opacity more dominant than in the byzantine power structure that the eu has established and is constantly building on. i rarely feel enthusiastic about the reports presented here in the european parliament. when i do, it is on reports on the internal market or on some environmental issue. the report by mrs dushana zdravkova is another exception. here, however, the image emerges that the ombudsman is working quite effectively to strengthen the individual citizens against the expanding byzantine empire. i am thinking of information to the public through a well-updated website, information sheets, networks with national and regional ombudsmen, conferences and, not least, meetings and other contacts with the eurocrats in the system to make them see that they are there for the citizens and not the other way around. at the same time, it is sad that the european power elite is running a european project that most people associate with the bad side of byzantium, the unmanageable bureaucracy. in the meantime, and pending a popular reaction to this development, we in the european parliament should strengthen the ombudsman. we should therefore vote in favour of the report by mrs dushana zdravkova and particularly welcome the requirement that the ombudsman should have full access to eu documents during his inquiries.
in writing. the commission is also proposing that the eu budget be limited to 1.00% of the average gnp of the member states. we have therefore voted against all the increases proposed by the european parliament, while junilista welcomed the few savings proposed by the committee on budgets or by individual members in the form of amendments. there are other unfortunate budget lines, but junilista particularly regrets the high level of support for the eu's agricultural policy, the cohesion fund, the fisheries sector and the budget lines which support various forms of information campaigns. the junilist also believes that the constant travel of the european parliament between strasbourg and brussels must be remedied and that the european economic and social committee and the committee of the regions should be abolished.

in writing. −we support the basic principles of the eu budget for 2009 and want to emphasise that it should give citizens good value for money. the framework set in the preliminary draft budget should be respected and we therefore welcome the fact that the budget will remain within this framework with a good margin. we want to cut agricultural and regional aid drastically and reduce the overall budget. we want to use the common money more for research and development, growth, infrastructure and security.
in writing. i abstained because a large part of the result is a disappointment. for example, it is crazy that the eu should finance aid to tobacco producers, while we are spending a lot of money on public health campaigns and anti-smoking campaigns in the eu. it is also a scandal that the eu budget should be used to support bullfighting, a tradition which i do not consider compatible with modern values and animal rights. i also regret that export aid of all kinds still has to be part of the eu budget and that the amendments aimed at strengthening trade union cooperation and consultation between industry and trade union organisations were not adopted by parliament.
in writing. −as always when voting on a budget the size of the eu, there are some details on which to comment. in the case of the eu, of course, the agricultural policy is the most prominent. it may therefore seem strange to vote for a budget where the dominant budget line is one that one would wish was one of the smaller ones - if any. at the same time, the big picture must be seen, and the good news is the growing awareness that much more resources must be devoted to what are really common tasks - in this case the climate. my vote should therefore be interpreted in the light of the fact that this year's draft budget is a 'case for progress', not as an uncritical endorsement of the overall content. two amendments to the voting record, including the introduction of tobacco subsidies.
in writing. −the report calls for increased interinstitutional cooperation through a general expansion of services within the eu institutions. the rapporteur believes that this will improve efficiency. for example, it is proposed to increase the staff resources of the political groups by 53 posts. in addition, two additional posts are being created in addition to the new posts included in the draft budget. junilista is very much in favour of making the eu system more efficient, but does not think that this is automatically achieved by more services! in principle, we are against an expansion, both of the eu budget and of more services, as we believe that this means bureaucratization and less national self-determination. as regards more posts in the political groups, we argue that the benefits of this will primarily be enjoyed by the large groups, which will make it difficult for other groups to pursue their own policies. in addition, the european parliament has already introduced eu grants to specific 'eu parties' and to political party foundations linked to them. we believe that this has done enough to benefit the major established political parties and their groups in the european parliament at the expense of the taxpayers. the junilista has therefore voted against the report.
in writing. - i have opposed the proposal since it was tabled in parliament, because i believe that the chicago convention, which today regulates the basic rules on air traffic charges, should continue to have the same significance for the member states. there is no need to change rules which are generally accepted and can therefore be regulated by the member states alone. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be given the right to vote on the proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work. i believe, however, that there is reason to be sceptical about the interpretation of binding community law by the court of justice. i am concerned about the reluctance to take account of national rules on certain matters. i believe that there must continue to be grounds for questioning the role of the court of justice, not least in this matter.
in writing. - i vote in favour of this resolution which calls for the problems of body scanning to be investigated before decisions are taken. the resolution could have gone further, however. i am opposed in principle to the use of body scans, which is an excessive restriction of privacy and disproportionate to the desired outcome. today's systems are already borderline privacy-invasive and sufficiently secure.
for the ind/dem group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. if we had a common literary reference framework in europe, i would start my speech with a quote from a great swedish poet: "you drown the voice of flattery: hear the truth once". the verse pattern is alexandrin with cesure. the two rapporteurs consider that monetary union has been a success. in doing so, they contribute to the myth-building around the euro that is not rooted in western critical thinking. the truth about the euro is different. first, its first decade has brought enormous costs in terms of lower growth and higher unemployment. secondly, monetary union has not been tested in the face of adverse weather until now. the research suggests that the impact on the volume of external trade could be quite large, perhaps even 3 to 4% of gdp. however, it turns out that the socioeconomic gains from this trade increase are very modest, perhaps 3 to 5 per cent of gdp, as a one-off gain. this negligible increase in prosperity has been achieved at the cost of the euro countries' inability to pursue an independent monetary and fiscal policy. germany entered this monetary union with a heavily overvalued currency and has lived with high interest rates and too tight fiscal policies. the cost has been enormous, and how are you doing now? the story that has been put together is that the eurozone countries should have come together forcefully and led the fight against the financial crisis. as everyone knows, this is a myth. it was the uk and gordon brown - who were outside the monetary union - who took the initiative. the eurozone followed suit.

for an excellent report. the past few months have shown the strength of the euro. few people thought ten years ago that the euro would be such a formidable success. some countries, including my own, months of financial turmoil, it is clear that only economic cooperation with the euro as the backbone can provide the security that the current global economic system requires. the fact that joint actions have led to a market clearing is proof of the strength of euro-cooperation. the euro should become the currency of europe. if that vision is to become reality, the already stringent convergence criteria should not be made even stricter. i therefore believe that it is wrong to make euro membership an exclusive club with higher entry requirements, as one of the amendments calls evident than ever, and hopefully also for nils lundgren. when the last financial crisis hit sweden in 1992, we could ultimately do nothing but let the krona fall. the lessons learned then led us to decide to become part of the european family. the swedish krona has lost value against the euro over the past year. in dealing with the financial crisis, sweden is outside both the protection afforded by the euro and the decisions that crisis management within the eurogroup decides on and requires. it is now as small countries that sweden should realise the value of a single currency. the stability offered by the euro provides the long-term prospects that are important for a country as export-dependent as sweden. sweden has a good economic development, it is true, but membership of the euro would have given us more stability in monetary policy and created more jobs, a more stable economy and stronger exports. the swedish parties should therefore be prepared to reconsider their passive attitude towards the euro as the currency of sweden. sweden should become a full member of the european union. it is therefore time that we in my country seriously began to discuss a new referendum. i myself hope for swedish membership within five years.

the rapporteur. - mr president! - what is it? the single market is a very important part of european cooperation, but for many consumers it is only a vague expression. regardless of how well we know its meaning, rules and regulations, we are all consumers who are all affected by the way the internal market works. i have always maintained that if we have satisfied and secure european consumers, we also have an efficient and thriving internal market. to win this trust, we must make the internal market more efficient and responsive to citizens' expectations and concerns. this does not necessarily mean more or stricter eu legislation and rules. information, training and self-regulation can often be a more appropriate and effective solution. whatever the solution, the aim must always be to ensure that consumers' rights are safeguarded, so that they can make good and informed decisions. this is of course also good for the market itself. the commission has also proposed that the commission should be given the task of drawing up a report on the impact of the single market on the consumer market. we believe that it can be an important tool for the future development of consumer policy. the single market covers some 500 million consumers and has a wide range of goods and services. it is of course impossible to examine in detail all aspects of the internal market. it is therefore important to use analytical resources where they are really needed most. i welcome the five areas on which the commission has focused: complaints, price level, satisfaction, exchange and security. these five main indicators are relevant and useful, although there is a need to develop, improve and possibly introduce new categories over time. i would also like to stress the importance of raising awareness of the scoreboard among consumers themselves and the general public. therefore, it is important that it be written in easy-to-understand language. in addition, the scoreboard should be visible on relevant webpages. finally, i would say that it will take time to develop the scoreboard, partly because we have come so far in different member states in terms of consumer policy and consumer protection, but also because we are different, have different cultures and different traditions. we have to be patient, we have to give this consumer scoreboard time. i would also like to thank commissioner meglena kuneva and her secretariat, as well as my own secretariat, for their very good cooperation.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the importance of fruits and vegetables to man is hardly news, much less that they are important foods for children. the fact that children are served fruit at school is a positive. it is good for your health and provides a much-needed boost of energy. but what does the proposal we are debating mean? it is a purely propaganda proposal on the part of the eu. the aim of this initiative is to win cheap political points while at the same time probably telling the children about the excellence of the eu. the committee on agriculture has put forward absurd proposals, including the presentation of european fruit as being better than any other fruit. only eu fruit, such as bananas from peripheral areas, shall be served. this is pure protectionism. children will be taught that the eu is good and that the fruit of the eu is even better. the commission and the european parliament should be ashamed of themselves.
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'excise' in the definition of excise duty. the reduction (a halving of previous indicative reference levels) is a step in the right direction towards a more responsible policy that takes public health seriously. we would point out, however, that we regard this as merely a first step towards a more ambitious policy in this area. we are also pleased that amendments nos 60 and 68 were rejected. this means that excise duties will continue to be levied in the country of destination.
in writing. - the junilist has voted in favour of the report, as we believe that it offers some opportunity to reconcile the requirements of national health policy with the aspirations of a free internal market. we have, however, voted against certain proposals with strong federalist overtones. junilistan considers it very important that, for example, swedish alcohol policy can be conducted in accordance with the values and decisions of the swedish parliament. this requires payment of excise duty in the recipient country for the distance sale of, for example, alcohol. this would not be the case if the amendments were adopted. on the contrary, it would mean, inter alia, that the provisions on acquisitions by individuals would be extended to cover also distance traders whose excise duty is paid in the member state in which the acquisition was made. as the cost of national public health problems such as alcohol and tobacco-related harm is largely borne by national tax revenues, the proposal for excise duty exemption from distance sales would hamper the ability of the public sector to deal effectively with public health problems. the problem also remains from a competitive point of view, since as a distance seller you can offer the same product as national operators, but much cheaper, only by avoiding the payment of excise duty in the same country. junilistan is in favour of competition, but believes that operators should compete on equal terms.
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'customs' in the context of the directive. the result of today's vote on the lulling report on the general rules for excise duties means that the guidelines for the import quotas for alcohol will be cut in half. in addition, the scope for duty-free sales is limited to ports and airports. the report also states that there are no obstacles to, for example, charging swedish excise duty on goods ordered from another eu country via the internet. the european parliament is only consultative on this matter, but today's decision is nevertheless an important milestone.
in writing. - there's no doubt that kids are good at eating fruit. apples, bananas and oranges prevent obesity and keep us healthy. in a way, it is therefore understandable that many today voted in favour of a proposal in the european parliament to subsidize schoolchildren's fruit in the eu. the problem is that responsibility for our fruit consumption does not belong at eu level. it is primarily the parents' job to teach their children good eating habits. the secondary responsibility lies with the municipalities and the thirdary responsibility lies with the state. as a federalist, i want decisions to be made as close to the citizens as possible. the eu wants that too. article 5 of the ec treaty provides that decisions which are better taken at a lower level are also to be taken at that level. that is why i voted against parliament's proposal to increase the fruit budget from eur 90 million to eur 500 million. in the eu, we will work for lower emissions, against crime and for increased mobility. more fruit, better exercise and less candy are issues that are being handled much better by schools, parents and local politicians.
in writing. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the common organisation of the market in fruit and vegetables is a very good one. the commission is also proposing to increase the ceiling on expenditure from eur 90 million to eur 500 million at the expense of the taxpayer. the committee stresses that only fruit originating in the community is covered. fruit of other origin is of no importance. the committee's proposal is ridiculous, as it suggests that the big brother should distribute seasonal fruit and encourage a wide variety of fruit so that 'children can discover different flavors'. the european parliament is once again taking up education policy. member states shall 'pedagogically integrate these measures into the school curriculum on nutrition and health'. the majority of this parliament has a distorted view of the cap. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover all the activities of the european union's institutions. thankfully, the european parliament has no co-decision power in these areas and should remain so.
in writing. in sweden, we tell our children that fruit is nature's own candy. i myself like fruit very much and i think it would be good if the children of europe ate a lot of these foods. i share the rapporteur's views on this. that said, the responsibility for ensuring that our schoolchildren get enough apples and bananas must rest with the parents of the children and possibly the municipalities where the schools are located. the european union shall not act as a supranational fruit police. instead, let us concentrate our energies and resources on more urgent tasks.
in writing. the european parliament has also adopted a resolution on the situation in the euro area. we welcome the emphasis on the social aspects of cooperation and the emphasis on growth problems. at the same time, we do not support paragraph 40 of the report, which states that non-euro area member states which meet the maastricht criteria and which do not have a derogation in the treaty should introduce the single currency at the earliest opportunity. we believe that this is beyond the powers of parliament to say. we respect the decision taken by the swedish people in a referendum and would like to stress that this is a matter to be dealt with in the respective member states.
in writing. − gender pay gaps are a major problem and something that individual employers and social partners have a particular responsibility to combat. this type of discrimination is contrary to fundamental provisions of the treaty and employers who fail to take this responsibility are already being prosecuted for it in court. however, it is contrary to our fundamental view of the swedish labour market and the responsibility of the parties, as well as current legislation, to create new legal instruments to control wage formation at eu level or through state wage policy. training for employment is not and should not be an eu competence. we have heard our demand to remove references to new legal instruments to regulate wage formation at eu level, and we have therefore voted in favour of the report as a whole. unfortunately, the report still contains a number of undesirable details, such as the proposal for 'one day for equal pay'. the recurring habit of parliament to demand the announcement of days, weeks and years for various events is a poster policy which makes no difference in substance. on the contrary, it tends to make the issue appear banal and frivolous.
in the light of the above, the commission has decided to propose a new regulation on the protection of the environment. increasing gender equality in the labour market, reducing the gender pay gap and equal pensions are important objectives in a just society. thus, the ideas of more statistics, revised legislation and educational efforts to counter slantrian views in society about gender are urgent. however, junilista is critical of the eu's seemingly insatiable need to integrate more and more policy areas into its remit. our basic view is that issues relating to the labour market system should be dealt with primarily by the individual member states and not raised at eu level.

of course not. but we do see a new structure in the global economy today, and the fact that the meeting in washington was a g20 meeting and not a g7 meeting is an expression of the new reality we are living in. i think that is good, because the whole dominance of the transatlantic economy is not there any more. in general, that is a good thing because it means that other parts of the world have been able to develop prosperity. it has, during this period, created imbalances: huge surpluses in countries such as china, and huge deficits in countries such as the us, coupled with a unique and artificially low interest rate in the us economy which has increased the credit volume more than ever. it is interesting to note that we have had the same debate in this chamber with colleagues from that side calling for lowering interest rates even further. had we done so, the problems in europe would have been even bigger than they are. now we need to see to it that we can restructure and recover the global economy, and i think that the european union has an important and crucial role to play in that. we need to make sure that we do what was said at the washington meeting, secure free trade and open markets and say ‘no’ to protectionism, because that would hinder recovery more than anything else. we need to make sure that the regulatory framework of the global financial markets catches up with the reality of how the world economy looks today. that is our task, and we must take a lead in that and develop prosperity even more.

president of the council. - madam president, these are serious issues raised by mrs goudin and mrs dahl. i can assure them, and assure your assembly, that the operations of the european union are conducted in accordance with the conventions on the protection of human rights and international law. this principle is clearly specified in all planning documents approved by the council and reflected in the individual instructions given to the forces on the move. it's what we call soldier's bodies in english. in the event that certain individuals deployed in foreign security and defence policy operations deviate from their obligations, disciplinary and judicial action shall be the responsibility of the member states. all the member states are parties to conventions on the protection of human rights. in the case in point, which is the subject of this question, let me take off my hat as president of the council and say this: in the interests of transparency, the french authorities, after having been in contact with the swedish authorities, have carried out a thorough investigation. this investigation was carried out by the inspectorate of forces in action and defence of the territory in respect of the french authorities. this investigation showed that the young man who was captured by french forces on 13 july 2003 during operation artemis in the democratic republic of congo was not tortured or abused. that is why the serious accusations against the french and swedish forces are unfounded.

this is something we should be allocating 25 billion euros to. why in god's name? are we doing that today? this is for those countries, the poor countries, i understand, which are in the eu but not in the monetary union and which may then have to be saved from a fate worse than death, balance of payments problems. this is a european union-created problem. what we're talking about here doesn't really exist anymore. my own country sweden is a country that is part of the eu, that is loyal and implements everything that is decided there much better than most other countries, but we are not with the monetary union. i say, that's reasonable. but if there were problems in sweden, would we have balance of payments problems? the answer is, of course, no. of course, one could imagine that sweden would start to neglect its economy, get a much higher rate of inflation and wage growth than other countries. what happens then, we get balance of payments problems? no, then the swedish krona will sink to compensate for this. otherwise, nothing happens at all. this is also true of other countries in the same situation, such as the united kingdom. what's the problem? yes, the problem is whether those countries which are in the european union - which they should be - but are not in the monetary union - which they should not be - should be forced, as you have suggested, to maintain a fixed exchange rate against the euro. the worst of all worlds: being forced to maintain a fixed exchange rate with its main trading partners. it is clear that if you mismanage your economy, have higher inflation or suffer structural setbacks in your main export industry, you will have reduced exports and increased imports. the question suddenly becomes: how will this be financed? but this is a completely artificial situation. it is totally out of date for countries not in a monetary union to choose to maintain an exchange rate and then have to be bailed out by the international monetary fund or by the eu or by someone else. why in god's name would you do this? it is a completely outdated approach to economic policy. either you join a monetary union - sometimes that may be true, i agree - or you stay out, stand on your own two feet, have your own independent monetary policy and manage your own affairs. if you're careful, nothing happens. if you're not careful, the currency goes down to compensate. it's not very dangerous, this one, either. i would therefore point out that when we are discussing here whether to allocate 25 billion euros to this purpose, it is an objective that is really not needed at all. it is a problem which we ourselves are creating, or rather you are creating. stop this. countries that are members of the eu but not of the monetary union must maintain a floating exchange rate regime. that'll take care of the problem.
madam president, i would like to thank you. it's jobs around europe that matter now. what we see in the automotive industry is an industrial challenge that also affects other parts of the european economy. what cannot happen now is that we have state aid which undermines employment in companies in one country but favours it in another. we cannot have state aid which disadvantages industry in one area and benefits it in another. because then unemployment and job losses will gradually spread across europe. we cannot have state aid that makes it difficult for viable companies to survive and that maintains companies that are no longer viable. the problem of the automotive industry is a long-term problem, we have seen more clearly now in the financial crisis, but we have seen around the world for a long time huge losses and declining demand, but also overcapacity. what is important now is to ensure that we have a demand in europe first. it is about tax cuts, about the possibility for european households to demand the products produced here. it is about ensuring that european industry can be a leader in technology and development in one area after another. in the field of environment as well as in the field of energy. that's where public efforts should go. but it is also about opening up the european automobile industry to be an international player. it is a question of ensuring that we can have open free trade where the european car industry has access to world markets. it is important to remember that those who are today making it difficult for free trade and for the success of the doha round are in fact stopping the european automotive industry from growing in the large markets of the future.

for the ind/dem group. president. - the debate is closed. scandals of corruption, fraud and irregularities have been common throughout the eu's history. public confidence in the eu is low. in sweden, we measure every year the swedish people's trust in different institutions. at the top, for example, we find health care, the police and the royal family, and at the bottom, politicians, trade unions and evening newspapers. at the very bottom are the european commission and the european parliament. the pattern is stable. so the eu needs an effective fraud authority. but we have had a bad experience with olaf: lack of independence, lack of transparency, hidden pressure on the appointment of the director-general and the monitoring committee. our rapporteur, ingeborg gräßle, has worked hard to ensure independence, transparency and strict compliance with the rules. i call on the house to give its full support to ingeborg gräßle's motion. it is an important first step in the eu's long march to win the trust of its citizens, if possible. let me end by making a particularly strong plea for an amendment which i myself have initiated. this requires all eu bodies to respect the protection of journalists' sources. after the scandalous tillacka affair, in which the olaf management itself is severely criticised, this is a much needed reform. it was finally the european court here in strasbourg that fully exonerated tillack last year. neither olaf, the european parliament nor the court of justice took any responsibility.

in writing. - the junilists consider it of great importance that the member states of the european area are economically prosperous and favour an independent european neighbourhood policy. we believe, however, that a common european aid system for medium-term financial assistance is neither a guarantee nor a solution to the problem of the lack of such a system. such a system creates an unnecessary and bureaucratic procedure whereby those member states in need of assistance are in fact placed in a position of dependence on emu countries with outside requirements for 'political and economic measures'. countries that are part of the european union - which they should be - but not part of the monetary union - which they should not be - are forced to maintain a fixed exchange rate against the euro and thus against their main trading partners. we therefore believe that it is unhealthy for countries which are not in a monetary union to choose to maintain a fixed exchange rate and then have to be bailed out by large regional and/or international bodies. the junilista therefore considers that the provision of eur 25 billion for balance of payments support to member states is unnecessary. instead, we are saying that the countries that are in the eu but not in the monetary union should maintain a regime of floating exchange rates. then this kind of problem will disappear and the taxpayers will save 25 billion euros!

mr president, i would like to thank you. the time for the climate negotiations, the eu climate package, is running out, and the responsibility for getting us there in time lies with the french presidency. parliament had already made its position clear in september. we will not accept a watered-down proposal. co-decision applies to everyone, including poland. the energy package is also being discussed. the energy package is very important for transparency and a functioning market. i would like to mention a few points, however, concerning emissions trading. first of all: those who let go will pay. that's why we can't compromise on an auction, and it would be completely insane to give everything away for free. secondly: if the measures force companies to move out of europe through carbon leakage, they will of course be compensated. however, a global agreement minimises this risk. that is why we should not be in a hurry to identify any victims before the copenhagen climate conference. i would point out that there are rules for small businesses and combined heat and power which are very important for industry. the money raised from climate action shall also be invested in climate action. that is why earmarking is needed. the poor countries are worried. we need money for mitigation and support there; we are 27, they are 77. in conclusion, i would like to remind our french colleagues of what general de gaulle once said: in politics, it's not the intention that counts, it's the result. we now have 24 hours with parliament and the trilateral meeting on emissions trading.
mr president, i would like to thank you. in march 2007, eu heads of government pledged that the eu would reduce its greenhouse gas emissions by at least 20% by 2020. it's a commitment that by itself is not enough to stop warming at the magic 2c limit that the research tells us is needed. yet it was a promise to our citizens and a step in the right direction. that is why i am very concerned to see that the instrument for achieving this goal, the climate package, is becoming weaker by the day. on monday, an informal agreement was reached between the french presidency and the major groups in parliament on how to reduce co2 emissions from cars. the result can be summed up very simply: business as usual for the automotive industry. it's completely incomprehensible to me. the commission already proposed in 1995 that emissions be limited to 120 g of co2. now, 13 years later, the case is being postponed even further. it is the result of all the loopholes in the legislation in the form of the phasing in of both the number of cars that have to comply with the requirements and ineffective penalties. i am also tired of hearing people use the financial crisis as an excuse for not taking responsibility for their climate impact. the automotive industry has had more than a decade to shift to a greener technology. this is not a result of the financial crisis, but it is the result of ongoing overproduction and a lack of conversion of the automotive industry. the negotiations on the emissions trading system are also in the final stages. it doesn't look bright for the climate here either. it's about the cdm projects. of course we should support climate projects in developing countries, but they have to be in addition to our own emissions reductions and not replace them.
mr president, while we are discussing reductions for 2020 of 20%, or at best 30%, more and more scientists tell us we have to do much more. the reason is the most recent science presented after the ipcc reports, and the message is that climate change is both more rapid and more serious than experts thought only a few years ago. the most alarming new information is about the interplay between the climate system, the oceans and the terrestrial eco-systems. oceans and vegetation on land have camouflaged warming by absorbing more than half of man-made emissions so far. this sink capacity is now at risk because of global warming, and it is further aggravated by the over-use and destruction of major eco-systems, in particular forests. while we can control emissions, we have no control over such positive feedback on the planetary system. so our effort, in my opinion, is too small. nevertheless we have to make a decision, hoping we can enhance our efforts step by step in cooperation with the rest of the world. with regard to the package, i am concerned that auctioning is being questioned. we need auctioning to stimulate innovation and to raise revenues for much-needed support for adaptation and green investments in developing countries. i am also concerned about the level of offsets proposed. in my opinion we are thus postponing the necessary technology transformation in our part of the world. i have been particularly involved in the negotiations on renewables. the most difficult area has been the sustainability criteria. here we had a breakthrough last night with the council on more ambitious greenhouse gas savings when using biofuels, and on including indirect land-use effects in the life-cycle analysis. i think that shows responsibility. let me make a final point. climate change is different from all other issues we are discussing. we can negotiate the detailed level of budgets, for instance, but we cannot negotiate with nature.
mr president, i would like to thank you. it is one of the most important tasks of europe and the european union to ensure that we now have an international commitment to take the global climate targets seriously. it will also be one of the most important tasks of the swedish presidency to ensure that we get this international commitment at the copenhagen meeting. i think it may be important when we discuss these issues to remember that success in copenhagen, with broad international commitment, means far more than the various details of the package we are now discussing. that is why it is so important to ensure that we now have a climate package in place that lives up to the targets we have set. i think it is important that we are pragmatic about the different means. if we are pragmatic with the different means and at the same time ensure that we achieve the objectives, we will also ensure that we have a broad consensus on what needs to be done and that there is a persistence in the will of the different member states to live up to the different commitments that exist. this is the important thing, and it is with this perspective that we are moving forward with regard to the different types of intervention. i would like to stress that we must also be clear about one thing, namely that we must always make room for the best-performing measures. this means that we must create space to invest in other countries where the results are greatest. this does not mean less demand on ourselves, but it does mean that we must be clear that what we can do to help in other countries must be in the foreground. finally, the resources now released by auction should be allocated to the member states for different measures in the different countries.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the council and parliament are negotiating the climate package. while it would be a great success for the partnership if we could get it in place before christmas, i have to say that i am very concerned about the direction of the negotiations. the latest research suggests that we would have to reduce emissions by more than the climate package requires. nevertheless, the council of ministers, especially since a number of right-wing governments are in power, is aiming to water down the package. it is also with sadness in my heart as a swede that i see that the swedish moderate government is one of the main actors in preventing the eu from taking the lead in the climate package. for several reasons, the majority of the eu's emissions reductions must be done at home: firstly, because we have a moral responsibility to reduce our own emissions; secondly, because it is by taking the lead and showing that it is possible to combine emissions reductions with high economic growth that we can get with other countries on an international climate agreement; thirdly, because we must have incentives in place for our industry to develop the new green technologies needed to be competitive in the future. i would therefore urge parliament to oppose the swedish government's position and insist that the bulk of the emission reductions be made within europe and also insist that part of the emissions trading revenue should go to the developing countries. that is how we will gain credibility, and that is how we can get an international climate agreement in place in the autumn of 2009 in copenhagen.

in writing. - high food prices primarily affect the world's most disadvantaged people. together with the financial and energy crises, the living conditions of large sections of the population are now at risk of deteriorating sharply. we recognize that the situation we have created calls for action. however, we do not share the rapporteur's ambition to set up another eu mechanism for the distribution of financial aid. development assistance, its scope, focus and content, is a good example of something that junilista believes should be decided at national level in the first instance and through cooperation through un agencies in the second instance. the junilist questioned the role of the eu, as it should be possible to seek solutions to the current food shortage through international fora instead. for these reasons, we have voted against the report as a whole.
